<p><b>Pope Pius IX</b> (<a href="/wiki/Italian_language" title="Italian language">Italian</a>: <i lang="it">Pio IX</i>, <i>Pio Nono</i>; born <b>Giovanni Maria Mastai Ferretti</b>;<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup> 13 May 1792 – 7 February 1878) was head of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> from 1846 to 1878, the longest verified papal reign. He was notable for convoking the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> in 1868 and for permanently losing control of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> in 1870 to the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a>. Thereafter, he refused to leave <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a>, declaring himself a "<a href="/wiki/Prisoner_in_the_Vatican" title="Prisoner in the Vatican">prisoner of the Vatican</a>".
</p><p>At the time of his election, he was seen as a champion of liberalism and reform, but the <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1848" title="Revolutions of 1848">Revolutions of 1848</a> decisively reversed his policies. Upon the assassination of his <a href="/wiki/Pellegrino_Rossi" title="Pellegrino Rossi">Prime Minister Rossi</a>, Pius escaped <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> and excommunicated all participants in the short-lived <a href="/wiki/Roman_Republic_(19th_century)" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Republic (19th century)">Roman Republic</a>. After its suppression by the French army and his return in 1850, his policies and doctrinal pronouncements became increasingly conservative, seeking to stem the revolutionary tide.
</p><p>In his 1849 encyclical <i><a href="/wiki/Ubi_primum_(Pius_IX,_1849)" title="Ubi primum (Pius IX, 1849)">Ubi primum</a></i>, he emphasized Mary's role in salvation.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> In 1854, he promulgated the dogma of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>, articulating a long-held Catholic belief that Mary, the Mother of God, was conceived without original sin.  His 1864 <i><a href="/wiki/Syllabus_of_Errors" title="Syllabus of Errors">Syllabus of Errors</a></i> was a strong condemnation against <a href="/wiki/Liberalism" title="Liberalism">liberalism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Modernism_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Modernism in the Catholic Church">modernism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moral_relativism" title="Moral relativism">moral relativism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Secularization" title="Secularization">secularization</a>, <a href="/wiki/Separation_of_church_and_state" title="Separation of church and state">separation of church and state</a>, and other <a href="/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment" title="Age of Enlightenment">Enlightenment</a> ideas. Pius definitively reaffirmed Catholic teaching in favor of the establishment of the Catholic faith as the state religion where possible. His appeal for financial support resulted in the successful revival of donations known as <a href="/wiki/Peter%27s_Pence" title="Peter&#39;s Pence">Peter's Pence</a>. He centralized power in the church in the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Curia" title="Roman Curia">Roman Curia</a>, while also clearly defining the Pope's doctrinal authority. His chief legacy is the dogma of <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> <a href="/wiki/Beatified" class="mw-redirect" title="Beatified">beatified</a> him in 2000.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life_and_ministry">Early life and ministry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life and ministry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Giovanni Maria Mastai Ferretti was born on 13 May 1792 in <a href="/wiki/Senigallia" title="Senigallia">Senigallia</a>. He was the ninth child born into the noble family of Girolamo dai Conti Ferretti (1750–1833) and Caterina Antonia Maddalena Solazzei di Fano (1764–1842).<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He was baptized on the day of his birth with the names Giovanni Maria Battista Pietro Pellegrino Isidoro. He was educated at the <a href="/wiki/Piarist" class="mw-redirect" title="Piarist">Piarist</a> College in <a href="/wiki/Volterra" title="Volterra">Volterra</a>, and in Rome. As a young man in the <a href="/wiki/Guardia_Nobile" class="mw-redirect" title="Guardia Nobile">Guardia Nobile</a> the young Count Mastai was engaged to be married to an Irishwoman, Miss Foster (the daughter of the <a href="/wiki/William_Foster_(bishop)" title="William Foster (bishop)">Bishop of Kilmore</a>), and arrangements were made for the wedding to take place in the <a href="/wiki/San_Luigi_dei_Francesi" title="San Luigi dei Francesi">Church of San Luigi Dei Francesi</a>. Mastai's parents opposed the marriage, and he did not appear at the church on the appointed day.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg/170px-Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="262" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg/255px-Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg/340px-Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg 2x" data-file-width="613" data-file-height="943" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_first_mass.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Illustration showing Mastai-Ferretti at his first Holy Mass in 1819</div></div></div>
<p>In 1814, as a theology student in his hometown of Sinigaglia, he met <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_VII" title="Pope Pius VII">Pope Pius VII</a>, who had returned from French captivity. In 1815, he entered the Papal Noble Guard but was soon dismissed after an epileptic seizure.<sup id="cite_ref-van_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-van-8">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> He threw himself on the mercy of Pius VII, who elevated him and supported his continued theological studies.
</p><p>The pope originally insisted that another priest should assist Mastai during Holy Mass, a stipulation rescinded after the seizure attacks became less frequent.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> Mastai was ordained a priest on 10 April 1819. He initially worked as the rector of the Tata Giovanni Institute in Rome.
</p><p>Shortly before his death, Pius VII – following Chilean leader <a href="/wiki/Bernardo_O%27Higgins" title="Bernardo O&#39;Higgins">Bernardo O'Higgins</a>' wish to have the Pope reorganize the Catholic Church of the new republic – named him <a href="/wiki/Auditor_(ecclesiastical)" title="Auditor (ecclesiastical)">Auditor</a> to assist the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Nuncio" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostolic Nuncio">Apostolic Nuncio</a>, Monsignore Giovanni Muzi in the first mission to post-revolutionary South America.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The mission had the objective to map out the role of the Catholic Church in Chile and its relationship with the state, but when it finally arrived in <a href="/wiki/Santiago" title="Santiago">Santiago</a> in March 1824, O'Higgins had been overthrown and replaced by <a href="/wiki/Ram%C3%B3n_Freire" title="Ramón Freire">General Freire</a>, who was less well-disposed toward the Church and had already taken hostile measures such as the seizure of Church property. Having ended in failure, the mission returned to Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Mastai had nevertheless been the first future pope ever to have been in America. Upon his return to Rome, the successor of Pius VII, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XII" title="Pope Leo XII">Pope Leo XII</a>, appointed him head of the hospital of San Michele in Rome (1825–1827) and <a href="/wiki/Canon_(priest)" class="mw-redirect" title="Canon (priest)">canon</a> of Santa Maria in Via Lata.
</p><p>Pope Leo XII appointed the 35-year-old Mastai Ferretti <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Spoleto" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Spoleto">Archbishop of Spoleto</a> in 1827.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> In 1831, the abortive revolution that had begun in Parma and Modena spread to Spoleto; the Archbishop obtained a general pardon after it was suppressed, gaining him a reputation for being liberal. During an earthquake, he made a reputation as an efficient organizer of relief and great charity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–19398-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The following year he was moved to the more prestigious <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Imola" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Imola">diocese of Imola</a>, was made a <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholicism)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal (Catholicism)">cardinal</a> <i><a href="/wiki/In_pectore" title="In pectore">in pectore</a></i> in 1839, and in 1840 was publicly announced as <a href="/wiki/Cardinal-Priest" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal-Priest">Cardinal-Priest</a> of <a href="/wiki/Santi_Marcellino_e_Pietro_al_Laterano" title="Santi Marcellino e Pietro al Laterano">Santi Marcellino e Pietro</a>. As in Spoleto, his episcopal priorities were the formation of priests through improved education and charities. He became known for visiting prisoners in jail, and for programs for street children.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193910_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193910-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>  Cardinal Mastai Ferretti was considered a liberal during his episcopate in Spoleto and Imola because he supported administrative changes in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> and sympathized with the nationalist movement in Italy.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Papacy">Papacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Papacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-weight: normal; background-color: gold;"><span style="font-size:80%">Papal styles of</span><br /><b><span class="fn">Pope Pius IX</span></b></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio_Nono.svg" class="image"><img alt="Pio Nono.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Pio_Nono.svg/60px-Pio_Nono.svg.png" decoding="async" width="60" height="70" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Pio_Nono.svg/90px-Pio_Nono.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Pio_Nono.svg/120px-Pio_Nono.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1264" data-file-height="1469" /></a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Style_(manner_of_address)" class="mw-redirect" title="Style (manner of address)">Reference style</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/His_Holiness" class="mw-redirect" title="His Holiness">His Holiness</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spoken style</th><td class="infobox-data">Your Holiness</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religious style</th><td class="infobox-data">Holy Father</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Posthumous style</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Cardinal Mastai Ferretti entered the papacy in 1846, amidst widespread expectations that he would be a champion of reform and modernization in the Papal States, which he ruled directly, and in the entire Catholic Church. Admirers wanted him to lead the battle for Italian independence.  His later turn toward profound conservatism shocked and dismayed his original supporters, while surprising and delighting the conservative old guard.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Election">Election</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Election">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/1846_papal_conclave" title="1846 papal conclave">1846 papal conclave</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Papal_conclave" title="Papal conclave">conclave</a> of 1846, following the death of <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_XVI" title="Pope Gregory XVI">Pope Gregory XVI</a> (1831–46), took place in an unsettled political climate within Italy. The conclave was steeped in a factional division between right and left. The conservatives on the right favoured the hardline stances and <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">papal absolutism</a> of the previous pontificate, while liberals supported moderate reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Carroll2010126_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Carroll2010126-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> The conservatives supported <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Lambruschini" title="Luigi Lambruschini">Luigi Lambruschini</a>, the late pope's <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Cardinal Secretary of State</a>. Liberals supported two candidates: <a href="/wiki/Pasquale_Tommaso_Gizzi" class="mw-redirect" title="Pasquale Tommaso Gizzi">Pasquale Tommaso Gizzi</a> and the then 54-year-old Mastai Ferretti.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg/180px-Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="192" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg/270px-Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg/360px-Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg 2x" data-file-width="722" data-file-height="769" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_portrait_illustration.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Illustration of Pope Pius IX soon after his election to the papacy in 1846</div></div></div>
<p>During the first ballot, Mastai Ferretti received 15 votes, the rest going to Lambruschini and Gizzi. Lambruschini received a majority of the votes in the early ballots but failed to achieve the required two-thirds majority. Gizzi was favoured by the French government but failed to get further support from the cardinals, and the conclave ended up ultimately as a contest between Lambruschini and Mastai Ferretti.<sup id="cite_ref-Pirie_–_Pius_09_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pirie_–_Pius_09-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>  In the meantime, Cardinal Tommaso Bernetti reportedly received information that Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Carlo_Gaetano_Gaisruck" title="Carlo Gaetano Gaisruck">Karl Kajetan von Gaisruck</a>, the Austrian Archbishop of Milan, was on his way to the conclave to <a href="/wiki/Jus_exclusivae" title="Jus exclusivae">veto</a> the election of Mastai Ferretti on behalf of the Empire of Austria and <a href="/wiki/Clemens_von_Metternich" class="mw-redirect" title="Clemens von Metternich">Prince Metternich</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBurkle-Young200034_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBurkle-Young200034-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> According to historian Valérie Pirie, Bernetti realized that he had only a few hours in which to stop Lambruschini's election. 
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Orsi,_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_(1847).png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png/220px-Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="318" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png/330px-Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png/440px-Giovanni_Orsi%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281847%29.png 2x" data-file-width="702" data-file-height="1014" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Orsi,_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_(1847).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Painting of Pope Pius IX, 1847</div></div></div>
<p>Faced with a deadlock and urgently persuaded by Bernetti to reject Lambruschini, liberals and moderates decided to cast their votes for Mastai Ferretti, in a move that contradicted the general mood throughout Europe. On the evening of the second day of the conclave, 16 June 1846, Mastai Ferretti was elected pope. "He was a glamorous candidate, ardent, emotional with a gift for friendship and a track-record of generosity even towards anti-Clericals and <a href="/wiki/Carbonari" title="Carbonari">Carbonari</a>. He was a patriot, known to be critical of Gregory XVI."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Because it was night, no formal announcement was given, just the signal of white smoke.
</p><p>On the following morning, the <a href="/wiki/Protodeacon" title="Protodeacon">Cardinal Protodeacon</a>, Tommaso Riario Sforza, announced the election of Mastai-Ferretti before a crowd of faithful Catholics. When Mastai Ferretti appeared on the balcony, the mood became joyous. Mastai Ferretti chose the name of Pius IX in honour of Pope Pius VII (1800–23), who had encouraged his vocation to the priesthood despite his childhood epilepsy. However, the new pope had little diplomatic experience and no curial experience at all. Pius IX was crowned on 21 June 1846.
</p><p>The election of the liberal Pius IX created much enthusiasm in Europe and elsewhere. "For the next twenty months after the election, Pius IX was the most popular man on the Italian peninsula, where the exclamation "Long life to Pius IX!" was often heard.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
<a href="/wiki/English_Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="English Protestant">English Protestants</a> celebrated him as a "friend of light" and a reformer of <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a> towards freedom and progress.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPougeois1877a215_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPougeois1877a215-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He was elected without secular political influences and in the full vigor of life. He was pious, progressive, intellectual, decent, friendly, and open to all.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193923_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193923-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> While his political views and policies were hotly debated in the comig years, his personal lifestyle was above reproach, a model of simplicity and poverty in everyday affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988357_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988357-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Governing_the_church">Governing the church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Governing the church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Centralization">Centralization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Centralization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg/220px-Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="218" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg/330px-Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg/440px-Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="793" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio_IX_e_Francesco_II_di_Borbone_ad_Anzio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pius IX in audience with King <a href="/wiki/Francis_II_of_the_Two_Sicilies" title="Francis II of the Two Sicilies">Francis II of the Two Sicilies</a> in 1862<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The most notable event in Pius IX's long pontificate was the end of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>, which lay in the middle of the "Italian boot" around the central area of <a href="/wiki/Rome,_Italy" class="mw-redirect" title="Rome, Italy">Rome</a>. In contrast, he led the worldwide Church toward an ever-increasing centralization and consolidation of power in Rome and the papacy. More than his predecessors, Pius used the papal pulpit to address the bishops of the world. The <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> (1869–1870), which he convened to consolidate papal authority further, was considered a milestone not only in his pontificate but also in ecclesiastical history through its defining of the dogma of <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988363_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988363-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Dispute_with_the_Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church">Dispute with the Melkite Greek Catholic Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Dispute with the Melkite Greek Catholic Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>After the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> concluded, an emissary of the Roman Curia was dispatched to secure the signatures of <a href="/wiki/Gregory_II_Youssef" title="Gregory II Youssef">Patriarch Gregory II Youssef</a> and the rest of the <a href="/wiki/Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Melkite Greek Catholic Church">Melkite</a> delegation who had voted <i>non placet</i> at the general congregation and left Rome prior to the adoption of the dogmatic constitution <i><a href="/wiki/Pastor_aeternus" title="Pastor aeternus">Pastor aeternus</a></i> on <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a>. Gregory and the Melkite bishops ultimately subscribed to it, but added the qualifying clause used at the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Florence" title="Council of Florence">Council of Florence</a>: "except the rights and privileges of Eastern patriarchs."<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> This earned Gregory the enmity of Pius IX; during his next visit to the <a href="/wiki/Pontiff" title="Pontiff">pontiff</a>, before leaving Rome, when Gregory was kneeling, Pius placed his knee on the patriarch's shoulder, just saying to him: <i>Testa dura!</i> (<i>You headstrong!</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In spite of this event, Gregory and the <a href="/wiki/Melkite_Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Melkite Greek Catholic Church">Melkite Greek Catholic Church</a> remained committed to their union with the Holy See.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ecclesiastical_rights">Ecclesiastical rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Ecclesiastical rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_(1898)_(14782675065).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg/220px-Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg/330px-Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg/440px-Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_%281898%29_%2814782675065%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1254" data-file-height="1882" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Italy_in_the_nineteenth_century_and_the_making_of_Austria-Hungary_and_Germany_(1898)_(14782675065).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cardinal Giacomo Antonelli, Pius IX's Secretary of State</div></div></div>
<p>The ecclesiastical policies of Pius IX were dominated by defence of the rights of the church and the free exercise of religion for Catholics in countries such as Russia and the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a>. He also fought against what he perceived to be anti-Catholic philosophies in countries such as Italy, Germany, and France.  The <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a> sought to  <a href="/wiki/Kulturkampf" title="Kulturkampf">restrict and weaken the Church</a> for a decade after the Franco-Prussian war. <sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECarroll2001479–494_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarroll2001479–494-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Jubilees">Jubilees</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Jubilees">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius IX celebrated several jubilees including the 300th anniversary of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a>. Pius celebrated the 1,800th anniversary of the martyrdom of the <a href="/wiki/Apostle_Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostle Peter">Apostle Peter</a> and <a href="/wiki/Apostle_Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="Apostle Paul">Apostle Paul</a> on 29 June 1867 with 512 bishops, 20,000 priests and 140,000 lay persons in Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939294_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939294-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> A large gathering was organized in 1871 to commemorate the 25th anniversary of his papacy. Though the Italian government in 1870 outlawed many popular pilgrimages, the faithful of <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a> organized a nationwide "spiritual pilgrimage" to the pope and the tombs of the apostles in 1873.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939297_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939297-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> In 1875, Pius declared a <a href="/wiki/Holy_Year" class="mw-redirect" title="Holy Year">Holy Year</a> that was celebrated throughout the Catholic world. On the 50th anniversary of his episcopal consecration, people from all parts of the world came to see the old pontiff from 30 April 1877 to 15 June 1877. He was a bit shy, but he valued initiative within the church and created several new titles, rewards, and orders to elevate those who in his view deserved merit.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939299_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939299-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Consistories">Consistories</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Consistories">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cardinals_created_by_Pius_IX" title="Cardinals created by Pius IX">Cardinals created by Pius IX</a></div>
<p>Pius IX created 122 new cardinals, of whom 64 were alive at his death, as compared to the limit of 70 living members allowed in the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a>. Noteworthy elevations to the "red hat" included <a href="/wiki/Vincenzo_Pecci" class="mw-redirect" title="Vincenzo Pecci">Vincenzo Pecci</a>, his eventual successor <a href="/wiki/Leo_XIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Leo XIII">Leo XIII</a>; <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_Wiseman" title="Nicholas Wiseman">Nicholas Wiseman</a> of Westminster; the convert <a href="/wiki/Henry_Edward_Manning" title="Henry Edward Manning">Henry Edward Manning</a>; and <a href="/wiki/John_McCloskey" title="John McCloskey">John McCloskey</a>, the first American ever to be elevated into the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988364_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988364-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to Bishop Cipriano Calderón, the pope intended to nominate the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Morelia" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Morelia">Bishop of Michoacán</a>, <a href="/wiki/Juan_Cayetano_G%C3%B3mez_de_Portugal_y_Sol%C3%ADs" title="Juan Cayetano Gómez de Portugal y Solís">Juan Cayetano José María Gómez de Portugal y Solís</a>, to the Sacred College in 1850 and even had Cardinal Giacomo Antonelli send a letter to him to express his intentions. However, the bishop died shortly before the letter was received in Morelia and had he lived, he would have been the first Latin American cardinal elevated five months later. According to the Benedictine monk Guy-Marie Oury, a letter addressed by <a href="/wiki/Prosper_Gu%C3%A9ranger" title="Prosper Guéranger">Prosper-Louis-Pascal Guéranger</a> to his Benedictine colleague Léandre Fonteinne on 6 March 1856 indicated that Guéranger had learned that Pius IX wanted to name him a cardinal in November 1855, but he refused the honor because he did not want to live in Rome. As a result, Pius IX included the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_La_Rochelle_and_Saintes" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of La Rochelle and Saintes">Bishop of La Rochelle</a> Clément Villecourt in his selection.<sup id="cite_ref-HRC_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HRC-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 22 August 1861, the pope informed the <a href="/wiki/Patriarch_of_Venice" title="Patriarch of Venice">Patriarch of Venice</a> <a href="/wiki/Angelo_Ramazzotti" title="Angelo Ramazzotti">Angelo Ramazzotti</a> that he would name him a cardinal, however, Ramazzoti died three days before the consistory. Also in 1861, the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Rota" title="Roman Rota">dean of the Sacred Rota</a> Ignazio Alberghini declined the pope's offer of nomination into the Sacred College. In December 1863, Pius IX intended to elevate the <a href="/wiki/List_of_archbishops_of_Gniezno_and_primates_of_Poland" title="List of archbishops of Gniezno and primates of Poland">Archbishop of Gniezno and Poznań</a> <a href="/wiki/Leon_Micha%C5%82_Przy%C5%82uski" title="Leon Michał Przyłuski">Leon Michał Przyłuski</a> to the cardinalate, but he died before the consistory took place. In 1866, Pius IX wanted to nominate a <a href="/wiki/Barnabites" title="Barnabites">Barnabite</a> to the College of Cardinals before he opened the First Vatican Council. While the pope originally decided on appointing Carlo Giuseppe Vercellone, a noted biblical scholar, Vercellone refused due to his precarious health, instead proposing that Pius IX instead nominate <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Bilio" title="Luigi Bilio">Luigi Bilio</a>. In 1868, Pius IX nominated Andre Pila to the cardinalate, however, he died the day before he would have been elevated as the only person for elevation in that April consistory. Also in 1868, Pius IX offered the cardinalate to the Bishop of Concepción José Hipólito Salas y Toro whom he had met during the First Vatican Council, inviting him to join the Roman Curia. However, the bishop preferred to live in Chile and declined the offer, while Pius IX did not offer it again in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-HRC_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HRC-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1875, Pius IX intended to nominate the papal almoner Frédéric-François-Xavier Ghislain de Mérode to the Sacred College, however, he died just eight months before the consistory was to be held. Pius IX also decided to nominate Augusto Negroni, but he declined and instead joined the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Society of Jesus</a> in mid-1874.<sup id="cite_ref-HRC_32-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HRC-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonizations_and_beatifications">Canonizations and beatifications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Canonizations and beatifications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pope Pius IX canonized 52 saints during his pontificate. He canonized notable saints such as the <a href="/wiki/Martyrs_of_Japan" title="Martyrs of Japan">Martyrs of Japan</a> (8 June 1862), <a href="/wiki/Josaphat_Kuntsevych" title="Josaphat Kuntsevych">Josaphat Kuntsevych</a> (29 June 1867), and <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_Pieck" title="Nicholas Pieck">Nicholas Pieck</a> (29 June 1867). Pius IX further beatified 222 individuals throughout his papacy, including the likes of <a href="/wiki/Benedict_Joseph_Labre" title="Benedict Joseph Labre">Benedict Joseph Labre</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peter_Claver" title="Peter Claver">Peter Claver</a>, and his two predecessors <a href="/wiki/Pope_Eugene_III" title="Pope Eugene III">Pope Eugene III</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Urban_V" title="Pope Urban V">Pope Urban V</a>.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Doctors_of_the_Church">Doctors of the Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Doctors of the Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Pius IX named three new <a href="/wiki/Doctors_of_the_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Doctors of the Church">Doctors of the Church</a>: <a href="/wiki/Hilary_of_Poitiers" title="Hilary of Poitiers">Hilary of Poitiers</a> (13 May 1851, naming him "<i>Doctor divinitatem Christi</i>" or "Doctor of the Divinity of Christ"), <a href="/wiki/Alphonsus_Liguori" title="Alphonsus Liguori">Alphonsus Liguori</a> (23 March 1871, naming him "<i>Doctor zelantissimus</i>" or "Most Zealous Doctor"), and <a href="/wiki/Francis_de_Sales" title="Francis de Sales">Francis de Sales</a> (19 July 1877, naming him "<i>Doctor caritatis</i>" or "Doctor of Charity").
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sovereignty_of_the_Papal_States">Sovereignty of the Papal States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Sovereignty of the Papal States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Papal_States_under_Pope_Pius_IX" title="Papal States under Pope Pius IX">Papal States under Pope Pius IX</a></div>
<p>Pius IX was not only pope, but until 1870 also the last <a href="/wiki/Sovereignty" title="Sovereignty">Sovereign</a> Ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>. As a secular ruler he was occasionally referred to as "king",<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAbout1859ch.&amp;nbsp;1_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbout1859ch.&amp;nbsp;1-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> though it is unclear whether the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> ever accepted this title. <a href="/wiki/Ignaz_von_D%C3%B6llinger" title="Ignaz von Döllinger">Ignaz von Döllinger</a>, a fervent critic of Pius' infallibility dogma, considered the political regime of the pope in the Papal States "wise, well-intentioned, mild-natured, frugal and open for innovations".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193945_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193945-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Yet there was controversy. In the period before the <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1848" title="Revolutions of 1848">1848 revolutions</a>, Pius was a most ardent reformer advised by such innovative thinkers as <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Rosmini" title="Antonio Rosmini">Antonio Rosmini</a> (1797–1855), who reconciled the new free-thinking concerning human rights with the classical <a href="/wiki/Natural_law" title="Natural law">natural law</a> tradition of the church's political and economic teaching on <a href="/wiki/Social_justice" title="Social justice">social justice</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> After the revolution, however, his political reforms and constitutional improvements were minimal, remaining largely within the framework of the 1850 laws mentioned above.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193947_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193947-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Reforms_in_the_Papal_States">Reforms in the Papal States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Reforms in the Papal States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a> and its <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Basilica" title="St. Peter&#39;s Basilica">Basilica</a> before Pope Pius IX added statues of Saints <a href="/wiki/St._Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Peter">Peter</a> and <a href="/wiki/St._Paul" class="mw-redirect" title="St. Paul">Paul</a></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:212px;max-width:212px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:129px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Rome_colosseum_engraving.jpg" //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Rome_colosseum_engraving.jpg/420px-Rome_colosseum_engraving.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="677" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">The centre of Rome showing the <a href="/wiki/Colosseum" title="Colosseum">Colosseum</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Forum" title="Roman Forum">Roman Forum</a> around 1870. Almost rural in character, it was known as the "Campo Vaccino" or "cattle field"</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Pius IX's liberal policies initially made him very popular throughout Italy. He appointed an able and enlightened minister, <a href="/wiki/Pellegrino_Rossi" title="Pellegrino Rossi">Pellegrino Rossi</a>, to administer the Papal States. He also showed himself hostile to Austrian influences, delighting Italian patriots, who hailed him as the coming redeemer of Italy. "They want to make a Napoleon of me who am only a poor country parson", he once declared.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In Pius' early years as pope, the government of the Papal States improved agricultural technology and productivity via farmer education in newly created scientific agricultural institutes. It abolished the requirements for <a href="/wiki/Jews" title="Jews">Jews</a> to attend Christian services and sermons and opened the papal charities to the needy amongst them. The new pope freed all political prisoners by giving amnesty to revolutionaries, which horrified the conservative monarchies in the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian Empire</a> and elsewhere.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222_15-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> "He was celebrated in <a href="/wiki/New_York_City" title="New York City">New York City</a>, <a href="/wiki/London" title="London">London</a> and <a href="/wiki/Berlin" title="Berlin">Berlin</a> as a model ruler."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222_15-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997222-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Governmental_structure">Governmental structure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Governmental structure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1848, Pius IX released a new constitution titled the "<a href="/wiki/Fundamental_Statute_for_the_Secular_Government_of_the_States_of_the_Church" title="Fundamental Statute for the Secular Government of the States of the Church">Fundamental Statute for the Secular Government of the States of the Church</a>". The governmental structure of the Papal States reflected the dual spiritual-secular character of the papacy. The secular or laypersons were strongly in the majority with 6,850 persons versus 300 members of the clergy. Nevertheless, the clergy made key decisions and every job applicant had to present a character evaluation from his parish <a href="/wiki/Priest" title="Priest">priest</a> to be considered.<sup id="cite_ref-Stehle_47_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stehle_47-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#What_information_to_include" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="A complete citation is needed. (March 2018)">full citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Finance">Finance</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Finance">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Financial administration in the Papal States under Pius IX was increasingly put in the hands of laymen. The budget and financial administration in the Papal States had long been subject to criticism even before Pius IX. In 1850, he created a government finance body ("congregation") consisting of four laymen with finance backgrounds for the 20 provinces.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Commerce_and_trade">Commerce and trade</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Commerce and trade">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius IX is credited with systematic efforts to improve manufacturing and trade by giving advantages and papal prizes to domestic producers of <a href="/wiki/Wool" title="Wool">wool</a>, silk and other materials destined for export. He improved the transportation system by building roads, viaducts, bridges and <a href="/wiki/Seaport" class="mw-redirect" title="Seaport">seaports</a>. A series of new railway links connected the Papal States to northern Italy. It soon became apparent that the Northern Italians were more adept at economically exploiting the modern means of communication than the inhabitants in central and Southern Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193952_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193952-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Justice">Justice</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Justice">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The justice system of the Papal States was subject to much criticism, not unlike the justice systems in the rest of Italy. Legal books were scarce, standards inconsistent, and judges were often accused of favoritism. In the Papal States and throughout Italy, organized criminal gangs threatened commerce and travelers, engaging in robbery and murder at will.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193949_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193949-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg/220px-Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="188" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg/330px-Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg/440px-Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg 2x" data-file-width="741" data-file-height="633" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_soldiers.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Papal soldiers around 1860</div></div></div>
<p>The papal army in 1859 had 15,000 soldiers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193950_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193950-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> A separate military body, the elite <a href="/wiki/Swiss_Guard" title="Swiss Guard">Swiss Guard</a>, served as the Pope's personal bodyguard.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Universities">Universities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Universities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The two papal universities in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a> suffered much from revolutionary activities in 1848 but their standards in the areas of science, mathematics, philosophy and theology were considered adequate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193953_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193953-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> Pius recognized that much had to be done and instituted a reform commission in 1851.
</p><p>During his tenure, Catholics and <a href="/wiki/Protestants" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestants">Protestants</a> collaborated to found a school in Rome to study international law and train international mediators committed to conflict resolution.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There was one newspaper, <i><a href="/wiki/Giornale_di_Roma" class="mw-redirect" title="Giornale di Roma">Giornale di Roma</a></i>, and one periodical, <a href="/wiki/La_Civilt%C3%A0_Cattolica" title="La Civiltà Cattolica"><i>La</i> <i>Civilta Cattolica</i></a>, run by <a href="/wiki/Jesuits" title="Jesuits">Jesuits</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193953_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193953-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arts">Arts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Arts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:442px;max-width:442px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:133px;max-width:133px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:195px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg/131px-Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg" decoding="async" width="131" height="195" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg/197px-Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg/262px-Pius_ix_with_jesus_and_angels.jpg 2x" data-file-width="874" data-file-height="1301" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">A hagiographic presentation of Pius IX</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:305px;max-width:305px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:195px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg/303px-Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg" decoding="async" width="303" height="196" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg/455px-Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg/606px-Lateran_basilica_illustration.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="697" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Illustration of the <a href="/wiki/Archbasilica_of_Saint_John_Lateran" title="Archbasilica of Saint John Lateran">Archbasilica of Saint John Lateran</a></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Like most of his predecessors, Pius IX was a patron of the arts. He supported architecture, painting, sculpture, music, <a href="/wiki/Goldsmith" title="Goldsmith">goldsmiths</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coppersmith" title="Coppersmith">coppersmiths</a>, and more, and handed out numerous rewards to artists.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193955_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193955-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Much of his efforts went to renovate and improve churches in Rome and the Papal States.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECapitelli201117–147_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECapitelli201117–147-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He ordered the strengthening of the <a href="/wiki/Colosseum" title="Colosseum">Colosseum</a>, which was feared to be on the verge of collapse.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193961_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193961-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Huge sums were spent in the excavation of Christian catacombs, for which Pius created a new archaeological commission in 1853.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Jews">Jews</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Jews">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Judaism" title="Pope Pius IX and Judaism">Pope Pius IX and Judaism</a></div>
<p>The Papal States were a <a href="/wiki/Theocracy" title="Theocracy">theocracy</a> in which the Catholic Church and its members had far more rights than other religions. Pius IX's religious policies became increasingly reactionary over time. At the beginning of his pontificate, together with other liberal measures, Pius opened up the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Ghetto" title="Roman Ghetto">Jewish ghetto in Rome</a>, freeing Jews to reside elsewhere. In 1850, after French troops defeated the revolutionary <a href="/wiki/Roman_Republic_(19th_century)" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Republic (19th century)">Roman Republic</a> and returned him from exile, the Pope reversed the Republic's religious freedom laws and issued a series of anti-liberal measures, including re-instituting the Jewish ghetto.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPougeois1877c258_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPougeois1877c258-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In a highly publicized <a href="/wiki/Mortara_case" title="Mortara case">case</a> from 1858, the police of the Papal States seized a 6-year-old Jewish boy <a href="/wiki/Edgardo_Mortara" class="mw-redirect" title="Edgardo Mortara">Edgardo Mortara</a> from his parents. A Christian servant girl unrelated to the family claimed she had informally <a href="/wiki/Baptism" title="Baptism">baptized</a> him during an illness six years prior, fearing he would die. This had made the child legally a Christian convert, and Papal State law forbade Christians from being raised by Jews, even their own parents. The incident provoked widespread outrage amongst liberals, both Catholic and non-Catholic, and contributed to the growing anti-papal sentiment in Europe. The boy was raised in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_household" title="Papal household">papal household</a>, and was eventually ordained a priest at age 21.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKertzer1998_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKertzer1998-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Policies_toward_other_nations">Policies toward other nations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Policies toward other nations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Pius IX was the last pope who also functioned as a secular ruler and the monarch of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States_under_Pope_Pius_IX" title="Papal States under Pope Pius IX">Papal States</a>, ruling over some 3 million subjects from 1846 to 1870. In 1870 the newly founded <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a> seized the remaining areas of the Papal States by force of arms. Contention between Italy and the Papacy was only resolved legally by the 1929 <a href="/wiki/Lateran_Treaty" title="Lateran Treaty">Lateran Treaty</a> (<i>Lateran Pacts</i> or <i>Lateran Accords</i>) between the Kingdom of Italy under <a href="/wiki/Mussolini" class="mw-redirect" title="Mussolini">Mussolini</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>, the latter receiving financial compensation for the loss of the Papal States and recognition of the Vatican City State as the sovereign independent territory of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Italy">Italy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Italy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Italy" title="Pope Pius IX and Italy">Pope Pius IX and Italy</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:392px;max-width:392px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:181px;max-width:181px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:144px;overflow:hidden"><a src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Political_manifestation_in_Rome_%28Speech_of_Pope_Pius_IX_from_the_balcony_of_the_Papal_Palace%29_by_Karl_Bryullov.jpg/179px-Political_manifestation_in_Rome_%28Speech_of_Pope_Pius_IX_from_the_balcony_of_the_Papal_Palace%29_by_Karl_Bryullov.jpg" //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Political_manifestation_in_Rome_%28Speech_of_Pope_Pius_IX_from_the_balcony_of_the_Papal_Palace%29_by_Karl_Bryullov.jpg/358px-Political_manifestation_in_Rome_%28Speech_of_Pope_Pius_IX_from_the_balcony_of_the_Papal_Palace%29_by_Karl_Bryullov.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2661" data-file-height="2144" /></a></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:207px;max-width:207px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:144px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Karl_Brullov_1850_Political_demonstration_in_Rome_in_1846.jpg" class="image"><img alt=""
