decoding="async" width="205" height="144" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Karl_Brullov_1850_Political_demonstration_in_Rome_in_1846.jpg/308px-Karl_Brullov_1850_Political_demonstration_in_Rome_in_1846.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fb/Karl_Brullov_1850_Political_demonstration_in_Rome_in_1846.jpg/410px-Karl_Brullov_1850_Political_demonstration_in_Rome_in_1846.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="704" /></a></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div class="thumbcaption">Two paintings by <a href="/wiki/Karl_Bryullov" title="Karl Bryullov">Karl Bryullov</a> showing the political situation of the time. (Left): Manifestation in Rome (Speech of Pope Pius IX from the balcony of the Papal Palace); (right): Demonstration in Rome in 1846</div></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg/220px-Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="153" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg/330px-Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg/440px-Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="832" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Perugia_massacre_patriots_1859.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Picture showing the massacre of <a href="/wiki/Perugia" title="Perugia">Perugia</a> citizens by the papal troops, 20 June 1859</div></div></div>
<p>Though he was well aware upon his accession of the political pressures within the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>, Pius IX's first act was a general <a href="/wiki/Amnesty" title="Amnesty">amnesty</a> for <a href="/wiki/Political_prisoner" title="Political prisoner">political prisoners</a>, despite the potential consequences. The freed revolutionaries resumed their previous political activities, and his concessions only provoked greater demands as patriotic Italian groups sought not only a constitutional government – to which he was sympathetic – but also the <a href="/wiki/Unification_of_Italy" title="Unification of Italy">unification of Italy</a> under his leadership and a <a href="/wiki/Italian_irredentism" title="Italian irredentism">war of liberation</a> to free the northern Italian provinces from the rule of Catholic Austria.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997223_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997223-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>By early 1848, all of Western Europe began to be convulsed in various revolutionary movements.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERapport2009_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERapport2009-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> The Pope, claiming to be above national interests, refused to go to war with Austria, which reversed Pius' popularity in his native Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997223_49-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997223-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> In a calculated, well-prepared move, <a href="/wiki/Pellegrino_Rossi" title="Pellegrino Rossi">Prime Minister Rossi</a> was assassinated on 15 November 1848, and in the days following, the Swiss Guards were disarmed, making the Pope a prisoner in his palace.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193935_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193935-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> However, he succeeded in escaping Rome several days later.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Roman_Republic_(19th_century)" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Republic (19th century)">Roman Republic</a> was declared in February 1849. Pius responded from his exile by excommunicating all participants.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDe_Mattei200433_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_Mattei200433-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> After the suppression of the republic later that year, Pius appointed a conservative government of three cardinals known as the <a href="/wiki/Red_Triumvirate" title="Red Triumvirate">Red Triumvirate</a> to administer the Papal States until his return to Rome in April 1850.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He visited the hospitals to comfort the wounded and sick, but he seemed to have lost both his liberal tastes and his confidence in the Romans, who had turned against him in 1848.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Pius decided to move his residence from the <a href="/wiki/Quirinal_Palace" title="Quirinal Palace">Quirinal Palace</a> inside Rome to the Vatican, where popes have lived ever since.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193945_34-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193945-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="End_of_the_Papal_States">End of the Papal States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: End of the Papal States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg/220px-St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg/330px-St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg/440px-St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1124" data-file-height="693" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St_peters_basilica_interior_drawing.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Illustration of the inside of Saint Peter's Basilica in 1870s, published by <a href="/wiki/John_Gilmary_Shea" title="John Gilmary Shea">John Gilmary Shea</a></div></div></div>
<p>After defeating the papal army on 18 September 1860 at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Castelfidardo" title="Battle of Castelfidardo">Battle of Castelfidardo</a>, and on 30 September at <a href="/wiki/Ancona" title="Ancona">Ancona</a>, <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_II_of_Sardinia" class="mw-redirect" title="Victor Emmanuel II of Sardinia">Victor Emmanuel II of Sardinia</a> took all the Papal territories except <a href="/wiki/Latium" title="Latium">Latium</a> with Rome and took the title <a href="/wiki/King_of_Italy" title="King of Italy">King of Italy</a>.  Rome itself was <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Rome" title="Capture of Rome">invaded on 20 September 1870</a> after a few-hours siege.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Italy instituted the <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Guarantees" title="Law of Guarantees">Law of Guarantees</a> (13 May 1871) which gave the Pope the use of the Vatican but denied him sovereignty over this territory, nevertheless granting him the right to send and receive ambassadors and a budget of 3.25&#160;million <a href="/wiki/Lira" title="Lira">lira</a> annually. Pius IX officially rejected this offer (encyclical <i>Ubi nos</i>, 15 May 1871), since it was a unilateral decision which did not grant the papacy international recognition and could be changed at any time by the secular parliament.
</p><p>Pius IX refused to recognize the new Italian kingdom, which he denounced as an illegitimate creation of revolution. He excommunicated the nation's leaders, including King Victor Emmanuel II, whom he denounced as "forgetful of every religious principle, despising every right, trampling upon every law," whose reign over Italy was therefore "a sacrilegious usurpation."<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mexico">Mexico</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Mexico">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Obelisco_Jamay.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Obelisco_Jamay.jpg/220px-Obelisco_Jamay.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Obelisco_Jamay.jpg/330px-Obelisco_Jamay.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Obelisco_Jamay.jpg/440px-Obelisco_Jamay.jpg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Obelisco_Jamay.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Obelisk in honor of Pope Pius IX in Jalisco, Mexico</div></div></div>
<p>In response to the upheavals faced by the Papal States during the 1848 revolutions, the Mexican government offered Pope Pius IX asylum, which the pope responded to by considering the creation of a Mexican cardinal and granting an award to <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Joaqu%C3%ADn_de_Herrera" title="José Joaquín de Herrera">President Herrera</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With French Emperor <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_III" title="Napoleon III">Napoleon III</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Second_French_intervention_in_Mexico" title="Second French intervention in Mexico">military intervention in Mexico</a> and establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Mexican_Empire" title="Second Mexican Empire">Second Mexican Empire</a> under <a href="/wiki/Maximilian_I_of_Mexico" title="Maximilian I of Mexico">Maximilian I</a> in 1864, the church sought relief from a friendly government after the anti-clerical actions of <a href="/wiki/Benito_Ju%C3%A1rez" title="Benito Juárez">Benito Juárez</a>, who had suspended payment on foreign debt and seized ecclesial property.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius blessed Maximilian and his wife <a href="/wiki/Charlotte_of_Belgium" class="mw-redirect" title="Charlotte of Belgium">Charlotte of Belgium</a> before they set off for Mexico to begin their reign.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> But the friction between the Vatican and Mexico would continue with the new Emperor when Maximilian insisted on freedom of religion, which Pius opposed. Relations with the Vatican would only be resumed when Maximilian sent the recently converted American Catholic priest Father Agustin Fischer to Rome as his envoy.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Contrary to Fischer's reports back to Maximilian, the negotiations did not go well and the Vatican would not budge.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Connor1971_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEO&#39;Connor1971-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Maximilian sent his wife Charlotte to Europe to plead with Napoleon III against the withdrawal of French troops from Mexico. After unsuccessful meetings with Napoleon III, Charlotte travelled to Rome to plead with Pius in 1866. As the days passed, Charlotte's mental state deteriorated.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> She sought refuge with the pope, and she would eat and drink only what was prepared for him, fearful that everything else might be poisoned. The pope, though alarmed, accommodated her, and even agreed to let her stay in the Vatican one night after she voiced anxiety about her safety. She and her assistant were the first women to stay the night inside the Vatican.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMichael2002_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichael2002-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="England_and_Wales">England and Wales</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: England and Wales">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>England for centuries was considered missionary territory for the Catholic Church.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988363_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988363-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> In the wake of <a href="/wiki/Catholic_emancipation" title="Catholic emancipation">Catholic emancipation</a> in the United Kingdom (which included all of Ireland), Pius IX changed that with the bull <i><a href="/wiki/Universalis_Ecclesiae" title="Universalis Ecclesiae">Universalis Ecclesiae</a></i> (29 September 1850). He re-established the Catholic hierarchy in England and Wales, under the newly appointed Archbishop and Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_Wiseman" title="Nicholas Wiseman">Nicholas Wiseman</a> with 12 additional episcopal seats: Southwark, Hexham, Beverley, Liverpool, Salford, Shrewsbury, Newport, Clifton, Plymouth, Nottingham, Birmingham, and Northampton.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877195_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877195-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Some violent street protests against the "papal aggression" resulted in the passage of the <a href="/wiki/Ecclesiastical_Titles_Act_1851" title="Ecclesiastical Titles Act 1851">Ecclesiastical Titles Act 1851</a>, which forbade any Catholic bishop to use an episcopal title "of any city, town or place, or of any territory or district (under any designation or description whatsoever), in the United Kingdom".<sup id="cite_ref-Act_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Act-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The law was never enforced and was repealed twenty years later.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ireland">Ireland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Ireland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius donated money to <a href="/wiki/History_of_Ireland_(1801%E2%80%931923)" title="History of Ireland (1801–1923)">Ireland</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Famine_(Ireland)" title="Great Famine (Ireland)">Great Famine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> In 1847 he addressed the suffering Irish people in the encyclical <a href="/wiki/Praedecessores_nostros" title="Praedecessores nostros"><i>Praedecessores nostros</i></a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Netherlands">Netherlands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Netherlands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Dutch government instituted religious freedom for Catholics in 1848.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> In 1853, Pius erected the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Utrecht" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Utrecht">Archdiocese of Utrecht</a> and four dioceses in <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Haarlem-Amsterdam" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Haarlem-Amsterdam">Haarlem</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_%27s-Hertogenbosch" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of &#39;s-Hertogenbosch">Den Bosch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Breda" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Breda">Breda</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Roermond" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Roermond">Roermond</a> under it. As in England, this resulted in a brief popular outburst of anti-Catholic sentiment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877205–206_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877205–206-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Spain">Spain</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Spain">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Traditionally Catholic Spain offered a challenge to Pius IX as anti-clerical governments came to power in 1832, resulting in the expulsion of religious orders; the closing of convents, Catholic schools and libraries; the seizure and sale of churches and religious properties; and the inability of the church to fill vacant dioceses.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877204_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877204-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> In 1851, Pius IX concluded a concordat with <a href="/wiki/Isabella_II_of_Spain" title="Isabella II of Spain">Queen Isabella II</a> stipulating that unsold ecclesial properties were to be returned, while the church renounced properties that had already passed to new owners. This flexibility of Pius led to Spain guaranteeing the freedom of the church in religious education.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877204_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877204-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_the_United_States" title="Pope Pius IX and the United States">Pope Pius IX and the United States</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg/220px-George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="263" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg/330px-George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg/440px-George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg 2x" data-file-width="628" data-file-height="750" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:George_Peter_Alexander_Healy_-_John_McCloskey_-_NPG.65.68_-_National_Portrait_Gallery.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pius IX elevated <a href="/wiki/John_McCloskey" title="John McCloskey">John McCloskey</a> as the first American to the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> on 15 March 1875</div></div></div>
<p>Pope Pius IX approved on 7 February 1847 the unanimous request of the American bishops that the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> be invoked as the <a href="/wiki/Patroness" class="mw-redirect" title="Patroness">Patroness</a> of the United States of America.
</p><p>Beginning in October 1862, the Pope began sending public letters to Catholic bishops of the United States calling for an end to the "destructive Civil War."  The Vatican never recognized the <a href="/wiki/Confederate_States_of_America" title="Confederate States of America">Confederate States of America</a> or sent any diplomats to it. However, in 1863 the pope did meet privately with a Confederate envoy and emphasized the need for emancipation.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> A letter of Pius IX to <a href="/wiki/Jefferson_Davis" title="Jefferson Davis">Jefferson Davis</a> in December 1863, addressing him as "Praesidi foederatorum Americae regionum" (President of an American regional federation), was not seen as recognition of the Confederacy, even by its own officials: Confederate Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Judah_P._Benjamin" title="Judah P. Benjamin">Judah P. Benjamin</a> interpreted it as "a mere inferential recognition, unconnected with political action or the regular establishment of diplomatic relations" without the weight of formal recognition.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius IX elevated Archbishop <a href="/wiki/John_McCloskey" title="John McCloskey">John McCloskey</a> of New York as the first American to the <a href="/wiki/College_of_Cardinals" title="College of Cardinals">College of Cardinals</a> on 15 March 1875.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Canada">Canada</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Canada">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius IX increased the number of Canadian dioceses from 4 to 21, with 1,340 churches and 1,620 priests in 1874.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939212_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939212-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Concordats">Concordats</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Concordats">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius IX signed concordats with Spain, Austria, <a href="/wiki/Grand_Duchy_of_Tuscany" title="Grand Duchy of Tuscany">Tuscany</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Portugal" title="Kingdom of Portugal">Portugal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haiti" title="Haiti">Haiti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ecuador" title="Ecuador">Ecuador</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>, <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988364_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988364-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Austria">Austria</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Austria">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/1848_revolution" class="mw-redirect" title="1848 revolution">1848 revolution</a> had mixed results for the Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>. It freed the church from the heavy hand of the state in its internal affairs, which was applauded by Pius IX. Similar to other countries, <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a> had significant anti-Catholic political movements, mainly <a href="/wiki/Liberalism_in_Austria" title="Liberalism in Austria">liberals</a>, which forced the emperor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Franz-Joseph I</a> in 1870 to renounce the <a href="/wiki/Concordat_of_1855" title="Concordat of 1855">1855 concordat</a> with the Vatican. Austria had already in 1866 nullified several of its sections concerning the freedom of Catholic schools and prohibition of civil marriages.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988362_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988362-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> After diplomatic approaches failed, Pius responded on 7 March 1874 with the encyclical <i>Vix dum a nobis</i>, demanding religious freedom and freedom of education.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Despite these developments, there was no equivalent to the German <a href="/wiki/Kulturkampf" title="Kulturkampf">Kulturkampf</a> in Austria, and Pius created new dioceses throughout Austria-Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939141–143_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939141–143-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG/220px-Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG" decoding="async" width="220" height="177" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG/330px-Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG/440px-Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1690" data-file-height="1356" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Expulsion_of_the_Russian_envoy_to_the_Holy_See_Felix_von_Meyendorff_by_Pope_Pius_IX.PNG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Expulsion of the Russian envoy Felix von Meyendorff by Pope Pius IX for insulting the Catholic faith</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="German_Empire">German Empire</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: German Empire">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Germany" title="Pope Pius IX and Germany">Pope Pius IX and Germany</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kulturkampf" title="Kulturkampf">Kulturkampf</a></div>
<p>In Germany, the state of Prussia, under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Bismarck" title="Otto von Bismarck">Otto von Bismarck</a>, saw Catholicism as a dangerous foreign influence, and in 1872–1878 fought hard to reduce the power of the pope and the bishops. After years of struggle in the <a href="/wiki/Kulturkampf" title="Kulturkampf">Kulturkampf</a>, the Catholics fought back by mobilising their voters in Prussia and in Germany as a whole. After Pius died, Bismarck came to terms with the new <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_XIII" title="Pope Leo XIII">Pope Leo XIII</a>. He dropped his alliance with the anti-Catholic Liberals and instead formed a political coalition with the Catholic <a href="/wiki/Centre_Party_(Germany)" title="Centre Party (Germany)">Centre Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Russian_Empire">Russian Empire</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Russian Empire">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Russia" title="Pope Pius IX and Russia">Pope Pius IX and Russia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Poland" title="Pope Pius IX and Poland">Pope Pius IX and Poland</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Pontificate" title="Pontificate">Pontificate</a> of <a href="/wiki/Pius_IX" class="mw-redirect" title="Pius IX">Pius IX</a> began in 1847 with an "Accomodamento", a generous agreement, which allowed Pius to fill vacant <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_see" title="Episcopal see">episcopal sees</a> of the Latin rites both in Russia (specifically the Baltic countries) and in the Polish provinces of Russia.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The short-lived freedoms were undermined by the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Church" title="Russian Orthodox Church">Russian Orthodox Church</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Polish political aspirations in the occupied lands,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and the tendency of imperial Russia to act against any dissent. Pius first tried to position himself in the middle, strongly opposing revolutionary and violent opposition against the Russian authorities and appealing to them for more ecclesiastical freedom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1934213–224_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1934213–224-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> After the failure of the Polish uprising in 1863, Pius sided with the persecuted Poles, protesting against their persecutions, and infuriating the Tsarist government to the point that all Catholic dioceses were eliminated by 1870.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877274ff_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877274ff-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Pius criticized the Tsar&#160;– without naming him&#160;– for expatriating whole communities to Siberia, exiling priests, condemning them to <a href="/wiki/Katorga" title="Katorga">labour camps</a> and abolishing Catholic dioceses.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He pointed to Siberian villages <a href="/wiki/Tunka_(village)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tunka (village)">Tounka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Irkutsk" title="Irkutsk">Irkout</a>, where in 1868, 150 Catholic priests were awaiting death.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShea1877277_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShea1877277-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Plans_to_leave_Rome">Plans to leave Rome</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Plans to leave Rome">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:212px;max-width:212px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:210px;max-width:210px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:157px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg/208px-Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg" decoding="async" width="208" height="157" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg/312px-Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg/416px-Pio_IX_affacciato_dal_treno_papale.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2670" data-file-height="2016" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Pius IX in a papal train, 1862. He was the first pope to be photographed.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:210px;max-width:210px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:122px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg/208px-Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg" decoding="async" width="208" height="122" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg/312px-Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg/416px-Inauguration_railway_bridge_san_paolo_1863.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2812" data-file-height="1650" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Pius IX in the inauguration of the "<a href="/wiki/Ponte_dell%27Industria" title="Ponte dell&#39;Industria">Ponte dell'Industria</a>" railway bridge, September 1863</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:210px;max-width:210px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:156px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg/208px-Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg" decoding="async" width="208" height="156" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg/312px-Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg/416px-Benedizione_di_Pio_IX_alle_Truppe_Pontificie.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption">Blessing of the Pontifical Troops, 1870</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Several times during his pontificate, Pius IX considered moving from Rome. On 24 November 1848, facing a rebellion by Italian nationalists, he fled to Gaeta in Naples, returning in 1850.
</p><p>Another occurrence was in 1862, when <a href="/wiki/Giuseppe_Garibaldi" title="Giuseppe Garibaldi">Giuseppe Garibaldi</a> was in Sicily gathering volunteers for a campaign to take Rome under the slogan <i>Roma o Morte</i> (Rome or Death). On 26 July 1862, before Garibaldi and his volunteers were stopped at <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Aspromonte" title="Battle of Aspromonte">Aspromonte</a>, Pius IX asked the British envoy Odo Russell if he would be granted political asylum in England after the Italian troops had marched in. Russell assured him of asylum if the need arose, but said that he was sure that the Pope's fears were unfounded.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERidley1976535_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERidley1976535-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> In 1870, after the <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Rome" title="Capture of Rome">Capture of Rome</a> and the suspension of the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a>, <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Bismarck" title="Otto von Bismarck">Otto von Bismarck</a> confided that Pius IX had asked whether Prussia could grant him asylum. Bismarck did not object, adding "it would be very useful to us to be recognised by Catholics as what we really are, that is to say, the sole power now existing that is capable of protecting the head of their Church. ... But the King [Wilhelm I] will not consent. He is terribly afraid. He thinks all Prussia would be perverted and he himself would be obliged to become a Catholic. I told him, however, that if the Pope begged for asylum he could not refuse it."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBusch1898a220_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBusch1898a220-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Theology">Theology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Theology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Theology_of_Pope_Pius_IX" title="Theology of Pope Pius IX">Theology of Pope Pius IX</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Portrait_pius_ix.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Portrait_pius_ix.jpg/180px-Portrait_pius_ix.jpg" decoding="async" width="180" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Portrait_pius_ix.jpg/270px-Portrait_pius_ix.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e2/Portrait_pius_ix.jpg/360px-Portrait_pius_ix.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1514" data-file-height="1973" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Portrait_pius_ix.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pope Pius IX (here pictured c. 1864) proclaimed two <a href="/wiki/Dogma" title="Dogma">dogmas</a></div></div></div>
<p>Pius was adamant about his role as the highest teaching authority in the church,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939313_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939313-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> as expressed in the dogma of <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">Papal infallibility</a> defined by the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> in 1870.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mariology">Mariology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Mariology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Marian_doctrines_of_the_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Marian doctrines of the Catholic Church">Marian doctrines</a> featured prominently in 19th-century theology, especially the issue of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> of Mary. During his pontificate, petitions increased requesting the <a href="/wiki/Dogma_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Dogma in the Catholic Church">dogmatization</a> of the Immaculate Conception.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2010)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> 
</p><p>In 1848, Pius appointed a theological commission to analyse the possibility for a Marian dogma.<sup id="cite_ref-Bäumer_245_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bäumer_245-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources#What_information_to_include" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources"><span title="A complete citation is needed. (March 2018)">full citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> On 8 December 1854, he promulgated the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_constitution" title="Apostolic constitution">apostolic constitution</a> <i>Ineffabilis Deus</i>, defining the <a href="/wiki/Dogma" title="Dogma">dogma</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Blessed_Virgin_Mary" class="mw-redirect" title="Blessed Virgin Mary">Blessed Virgin Mary</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Encyclicals">Encyclicals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Encyclicals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_encyclicals_of_Pope_Pius_IX" title="List of encyclicals of Pope Pius IX">List of encyclicals of Pope Pius IX</a></div>
<p>Pius issued a record 38 <a href="/wiki/Encyclical" title="Encyclical">encyclicals</a>. They include:
</p>
<ul><li><i><a href="/wiki/Qui_pluribus" title="Qui pluribus">Qui pluribus</a></i> 1846, his first encyclical, on faith and religion</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Praedecessores_nostros" title="Praedecessores nostros">Praedecessores nostros</a></i> 1847 on aid for Ireland</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Ubi_primum_(Pius_IX,_1849)" title="Ubi primum (Pius IX, 1849)">Ubi primum</a></i> 1848 on <a href="/wiki/The_Immaculate_Conception" class="mw-redirect" title="The Immaculate Conception">The Immaculate Conception</a></li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Nostis_et_nobiscum" title="Nostis et nobiscum">Nostis et nobiscum</a></i> 1849 on the church in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a></li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Neminem_vestrum&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Neminem vestrum (page does not exist)">Neminem vestrum</a></i> 1854 on the bloody persecution of Armenians</li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cum_nuper&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cum nuper (page does not exist)">Cum nuper</a></i> 1858 on care for clerics</li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Amantissimus&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Amantissimus (page does not exist)">Amantissimus</a></i> 1862 on care of the churches</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Ad_universalis_Ecclesiae" title="Ad universalis Ecclesiae">Ad universalis Ecclesiae</a> 1862</i> on conditions for admission to male religious orders in which <a href="/wiki/Simple_vow" class="mw-redirect" title="Simple vow">solemn vows</a> are prescribed</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Quanta_cura" title="Quanta cura">Quanta cura</a></i> 1864, with its appendix the <i><a href="/wiki/Syllabus_of_Errors" title="Syllabus of Errors">Syllabus of Errors</a></i></li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Meridionali_Americae&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Meridionali Americae (page does not exist)">Meridionali Americae</a></i> 1865 on the Seminary for the Native Clergy</li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Omnem_sollicitudinem&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Omnem sollicitudinem (page does not exist)">Omnem sollicitudinem</a></i> 1874 on the Greek-Ruthenian Rite</li>
<li><i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Quod_nunquam&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Quod nunquam (page does not exist)">Quod nunquam</a></i> 1875 on the Church in <a href="/wiki/Prussia" title="Prussia">Prussia</a></li></ul>
<p>Unlike popes in the 20th century, Pius IX did not use encyclicals to explain the faith, but to condemn what he considered errors. He was the first pope to popularize encyclicals on a large scale to foster his views.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="First_Vatican_Council">First Vatican Council</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: First Vatican Council">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican,_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg/220px-The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg/330px-The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg/440px-The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican%2C_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7986" data-file-height="6198" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:The_oecumenical_council_of_the_Vatican,_convened_December_8th_1869_LCCN2006677480.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> presided over by Pius IX, 1869</div></div></div>
<p>After prior consultation of the hierarchy in <i><a href="/wiki/Ubi_primum_(Pius_IX,_1849)" title="Ubi primum (Pius IX, 1849)">Ubi primum</a></i> (see above), Pius decisively acted on the century-old disagreement between Dominicans and Franciscans regarding the Immaculate Conception of Mary, deciding in favour of the Franciscan view.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988340_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988340-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>However, his defining this infallible <a href="/wiki/Dogma" title="Dogma">dogma</a> raised a question: Can a pope make such decisions without the authority of the bishops? This doctrine of <a href="/wiki/Papal_infallibility" title="Papal infallibility">papal infallibility</a>, enhancing the role of the papacy and decreasing the role of the bishops, became a topic of the <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a> convened in 1869.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988340_87-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzenBäumer1988340-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Institutions">Institutions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Institutions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Pius IX approved 74 new religious congregations for women alone. In France, he created over 200 new dioceses and created new hierarchies in several countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997324_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997324-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> He supported Catholic associations such as the Ambrosian Circle in Italy, the Union of Catholic Workers in France, and the Pius Verein and the Deutsche Katholische Gesellschaft in Germany, whose purpose was to bring the fullness of Catholic faith to people outside the church.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939313–315_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939313–315-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_years_and_death">Later years and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Later years and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg/200px-Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="291" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg/300px-Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg/400px-Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1080" data-file-height="1570" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_ix_%E2%80%93_edited.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Portrait of Pius IX taken in 1871</div></div></div>
<p>Since 1868, the pope had been plagued first by facial <a href="/wiki/Erysipelas" title="Erysipelas">erysipelas</a> and then by open sores on his legs.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Nevertheless, he insisted on celebrating daily Mass. The extraordinary heat of the summer of 1877 worsened the sores to the effect that he had to be carried. He underwent several painful medical procedures with remarkable patience.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He spent most of his last few weeks in his library, where he received cardinals and held <a href="/wiki/Papal_audience" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal audience">papal audiences</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939101_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939101-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> On 8 December, the Feast of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>, his situation improved markedly to the point that he could walk again.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg/220px-Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="140" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg/330px-Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg/440px-Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1490" data-file-height="948" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pius_IX_-_funeral.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Illustration of the funeral of Pius IX at Saint Peter's Basilica, published by <a href="/wiki/Currier_and_Ives" title="Currier and Ives">Currier and Ives</a> in 1878</div></div></div>
<p>By February, he could say Mass again on his own in a standing position, enjoying the popular celebration of the 75th anniversary of his <a href="/wiki/First_Communion" title="First Communion">First Communion</a>. <a href="/wiki/Bronchitis" title="Bronchitis">Bronchitis</a>, a fall to the floor, and rising temperature worsened his situation after 4 February 1878. He continued joking about himself: when the Cardinal Vicar of Rome ordered bell-ringing and non-stop prayers for his recuperation, the pope asked, "Why do you want to stop me from going to heaven?" He told his doctor that his time had come.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939102_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939102-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pius IX lived just long enough to witness the death of his old adversary, <a href="/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_II_of_Italy" class="mw-redirect" title="Victor Emmanuel II of Italy">Victor Emmanuel II of Italy</a>, in January 1878. As soon as he learned about the seriousness of the situation of the king, he absolved him of all <a href="/wiki/Excommunication" title="Excommunication">excommunications</a> and other ecclesiastical punishments. Pius IX died one month later on 7 February 1878 at 5:40&#160;pm, aged 85, while saying the <a href="/wiki/Rosary" title="Rosary">rosary</a> with his staff. The cause of death was <a href="/wiki/Epilepsy" title="Epilepsy">epilepsy</a>, which led to a seizure and a sudden <a href="/wiki/Myocardial_infarction" title="Myocardial infarction">heart attack</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939100–102_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–1939100–102-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> His last words were, "Guard the Church I loved so well and sacredly", as recorded by the cardinals kneeling beside his bedside.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> His death concluded the second-longest pontificate in papal history, after that of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a>, who tradition holds had reigned for 37 years.
</p><p>His body was originally buried in Saint Peter's grotto, but was moved in a night procession on 13 July 1881 to the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_di_San_Lorenzo_fuori_le_Mura" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica di San Lorenzo fuori le Mura">Basilica of Saint Lawrence outside the Walls</a>. When the cortege approached the Tiber River, a group of anticlerical Romans screaming "Long live Italy! Death to the Pope! Death to the Priests!" threatened to throw the coffin into the river but a contingent of militia arrived to prevent this.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKelly1987310Schmidlin1922–1939103–104_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly1987310Schmidlin1922–1939103–104-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> The simple grave of Pius IX was changed by his successor John Paul II after his <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beatification">Beatification</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Beatification">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Blessed</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Pius IX</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:G.P.A.Healy,_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_(1871).jpg" class="image"><img alt="G.P.A.Healy, Portrait of Pope Pius IX (1871).jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg/220px-G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg/330px-G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg/440px-G.P.A.Healy%2C_Portrait_of_Pope_Pius_IX_%281871%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a><div class="infobox-caption">Portrait by <a href="/wiki/George_Peter_Alexander_Healy" title="George Peter Alexander Healy">George Peter Alexander Healy</a>, 1871 <small>(oil on canvas, 73.6&#160;×&#160;43.1&#160;cm; 	
Museo Pio IX)</small></div></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;"><a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a>; <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">Giovanni Maria Battista Pietro Pellegrino Isidoro Mastai-Ferretti<br />13 May 1792<br /><a href="/wiki/Senigallia" title="Senigallia">Senigallia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Province_of_Ancona" title="Province of Ancona">Ancona</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marche" title="Marche">Marche</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">7 February 1878 (aged 85)<br /><a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Palace" title="Apostolic Palace">Apostolic Palace</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">Beatified</a></th><td class="infobox-data">3 September 2000, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter%27s_Square" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Peter&#39;s Square">Saint Peter's Square</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">7 February</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Papal_vestments" class="mw-redirect" title="Papal vestments">Papal attire</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Papal_tiara" title="Papal tiara">Papal tiara</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Senigallia" title="Senigallia">Senigallia</a><br /><a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">First Vatican Council</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Senigallia" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Senigallia">Diocese of Senigallia</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg/200px-Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="129" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg/300px-Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg/400px-Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5772" data-file-height="3726" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tomb_of_Pope_Pius_IX.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tomb of Pius IX at San Lorenzo fuori le Mura</div></div></div>
<p>The process for his <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatification</a>, which in the early stages was strongly opposed by the Italian government, was begun on 11 February 1907, and recommenced three times.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWoodward1996310–11_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodward1996310–11-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> The Italian government had since 1878 strongly opposed beatification of Pius IX. Without Italian opposition, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> declared Pius IX to be <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">Venerable</a> on 6 July 1985 (upon confirming his life of <a href="/wiki/Heroic_virtue" title="Heroic virtue">heroic virtue</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Beatification" title="Beatification">beatified</a> him on 3 September 2000 (his annual liturgical commemoration is 7 February, the date of his death).
</p><p>The beatification of Pius IX was controversial and was criticized by some Jews and Christians because of what was perceived as his authoritarian, reactionary politics; the accusation of abuse of episcopal powers; and <a href="/wiki/Antisemitism" title="Antisemitism">antisemitism</a> (most specifically the case of <a href="/wiki/Edgardo_Mortara" class="mw-redirect" title="Edgardo Mortara">Edgardo Mortara</a>, but also his reinstituting the Roman ghetto).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMilavec2007159–160_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMilavec2007159–160-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Pius IX celebrated his silver jubilee in 1871, going on to have the <a href="/wiki/List_of_popes_by_length_of_reign" class="mw-redirect" title="List of popes by length of reign">longest reign in the history of the post-apostolic papacy</a>, 31 years, 7 months, and 23 days. As his temporal sovereignty was lost, the Church rallied around him, and the papacy became more centralized, encouraged by his personal habits of simplicity.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991336ff_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFranzen1991336ff-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Pius IX's pontificate marks the beginning of the modern papacy: from his time on, it has become increasingly a spiritual rather than temporal authority.
</p><p>Having started as a liberal, Pius IX turned conservative after being chased from Rome. Thereafter, he was considered politically conservative, but a restless and radical reformer and innovator of Church life and structures. Church life, religious vocations, new foundations and religious enthusiasm all flourished at the end of his pontificate.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997324Schmidlin1922–1939292ff_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuffy1997324Schmidlin1922–1939292ff-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Politically, he suffered the isolation of the papacy from most major world powers: "the prisoner of the Vatican" had poor relations with <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX_and_Russia" title="Pope Pius IX and Russia">Russia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kulturkampf" title="Kulturkampf">Germany</a>, the United States, and France, and open hostility with Italy. Yet he was most popular with the remaining Catholic faithful in all these countries, in many of which Pope Pius associations were formed in his support.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> He made lasting ecclesiastical history with his 1854 <a href="/wiki/Infallible" class="mw-redirect" title="Infallible">infallible</a> decision of the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>, which was the basis for the later dogma on the <a href="/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary" title="Assumption of Mary">Assumption</a>. His other lasting contribution is the invocation of the <a href="/wiki/Ecumenical_council" title="Ecumenical council">ecumenical council</a> <a href="/wiki/First_Vatican_Council" title="First Vatican Council">Vatican One</a>, which promulgated the definition of Papal <a href="/wiki/Infallibility" title="Infallibility">infallibility</a>. With his advice he helped <a href="/wiki/John_Bosco" title="John Bosco">John Bosco</a> found the <a href="/wiki/Salesians_of_Don_Bosco" title="Salesians of Don Bosco">Salesian Society</a>, for which reason he is also called "don Bosco's Pope".<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>In two nights after his 1846 pardon freeing all political prisoners, thousands of Romans with torches roamed to the <a href="/wiki/Quirinal_Palace" title="Quirinal Palace">Quirinal Palace</a>, where Pius IX lived, celebrating the pope with <i>Evvivas</i>, speeches and music through both nights. The Pope went several times to the balcony to give his blessing. On the third day, when his horse-drawn carriage left the Palace to move to the Vatican, Romans unhitched the horses and pulled the papal carriage on their own.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193926_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193926-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>On 16 November 1848, a crowd of revolutionaries moved to the Quirinal and the Parliament to present to the Pope their demands, especially war against Austria. The Pope reportedly replied, his dignity as head of state and of the church does not permit him to fulfil conditions of rebels. Following this, the Quirinal was covered by cannon fire, which caused several deaths. After that, to save lives, the Pope agreed to a list of proposed ministers, although stating that he would abstain from any cooperation with them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193929ff_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193929ff-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>After the French troops, who had previously protected the Papal States, left Rome, an Italian army with 60,000 men approached the city, which was defended by only 10,000 papal soldiers. The Pope instructed his hopelessly outnumbered soldiers to give only token resistance and to enter an armistice after the first defeat because the Deputy of Christ does not shed blood. When the old <a href="/wiki/Porta_Pia" title="Porta Pia">Porta Pia</a> was bombarded, opening a huge hole for the invaders, the Pope asked the white flag to be shown. It was his last act as King of the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193989_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESchmidlin1922–193989-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The last papal shot at the Porta Pia was fired by an Austrian alumnus of the <a href="/wiki/Stella_Matutina_(Jesuit_school)" title="Stella Matutina (Jesuit school)">Stella Matutina</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKnünz1956_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKnünz1956-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Pius IX was lampooned in a pun on the Italian version of his name (<i>Pio Nono</i> – <i>Nono</i> meaning "Ninth"), as <i>Pio No No</i>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>His occasional mood changes and emotional outbursts have been interpreted as symptoms of his <a href="/wiki/Epilepsy" title="Epilepsy">epilepsy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESirvenDrazkowskiNoe2007_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESirvenDrazkowskiNoe2007-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag failed verification of its source citation(s). (March 2018)">failed verification</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>One enduring popular touch lies in Pius IX's artistic legacy as author of the Italian-language lyrics of Italy's best-known indigenous Christmas carol, "<a href="/wiki/Tu_scendi_dalle_stelle" title="Tu scendi dalle stelle">Tu scendi dalle stelle</a>" ("From starry skies descended"), originally a <a href="/wiki/Neapolitan_language" title="Neapolitan language">Neapolitan language</a> song written by <a href="/wiki/Alphonsus_Liguori" title="Alphonsus Liguori">Alphonsus Liguori</a>.</li>
<li>During his stay at the Kingdom of <a href="/wiki/Two_Sicilies" class="mw-redirect" title="Two Sicilies">Two Sicilies</a>, on 8 September 1849, Pope Pius IX had the experience of a train trip from <a href="/wiki/Portici" title="Portici">Portici</a> to <a href="/wiki/Pagani,_Campania" title="Pagani, Campania">Pagani</a>, so he became enthusiastic about this modern invention. When he went back to his seat in Rome, he promoted the growth of a railroad network, starting in 1856 with the <a href="/wiki/Rome_and_Frascati_Rail_Road" class="mw-redirect" title="Rome and Frascati Rail Road">Rome and Frascati Rail Road</a>. By 1870, the length of railway lines built in the <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> was 317 kilometres (197&#160;mi). He also introduced gas lighting and the telegraph to the Papal States.</li>
<li>To commemorate his term as pope, a <a href="/wiki/Montreal" title="Montreal">Montreal</a> street is called <a href="/wiki/Pie-IX_Boulevard" title="Pie-IX Boulevard">Pie-IX Boulevard</a> (Pie-Neuf). There is also a stop on <a href="/wiki/Montreal_Metro" title="Montreal Metro">Montreal Metro</a> system called <a href="/wiki/Pie-IX_(Montreal_Metro)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pie-IX (Montreal Metro)">Pie-IX</a> serving the <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Stadium_(Montreal)" title="Olympic Stadium (Montreal)">Olympic Stadium</a>, which is located alongside Pie-IX Boulevard. In addition, streets in <a href="/wiki/Santiago,_Chile" class="mw-redirect" title="Santiago, Chile">Santiago, Chile</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Macon,_Georgia" title="Macon, Georgia">Macon</a>, Georgia, are called Pío Nono, Italian for Pius IX, and a secondary school has the same name (Pio IX) in Buenos Aires, Argentina. Various sweets in <a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>, <a href="/wiki/Latin_America" title="Latin America">Latin America</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> are also named <a href="/wiki/Pionono" title="Pionono">piononos</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In the <a href="/wiki/Luigi_Magni" title="Luigi Magni">Luigi Magni</a> film <i><a href="/wiki/In_the_Name_of_the_Sovereign_People" title="In the Name of the Sovereign People">In the Name of the Sovereign People</a></i> (1990), Pius IX is played by <a href="/wiki/Gianni_Bonagura" title="Gianni Bonagura">Gianni Bonagura</a>.</li></ul>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Episcopal_lineage">Episcopal lineage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Pope_Pius_IX&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Episcopal lineage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
